Everything just feels like too much right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any panic attacks since the new operations manager started?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't had anything like that. I mean, I get really stressed out and wound up, but... no, no actual panic attacks. Just a lot of worry.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or depressed most of the time since the new operations manager started?\n\nPatient: No, not really depressed. I mean, I'm definitely frustrated and overwhelmed by the whole situation, but I wouldn't say I feel sad most of the time. It's more just this constant tension and worrying about work, not a deep sadness.\n\nDoctor: Have you been repeating certain behaviors or mental acts in response to the stress at work?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed anything like that. I'm not doing anything over and over again, or... having to think certain things to cope. It's really just the stress and the avoiding things I mentioned before.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific about the new operations manager or the changes they've made that you think is causing you the most distress?\n\nPatient: I don't know if it's one specific thing. It's just... the whole way they run things now. Everything is different, the expectations feel impossible, and I just can't seem to get a handle on it. It's just the constant pressure of it all, I guess.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I come home completely wound up, I end up snapping at my husband, and I just lie awake at night replaying all these meetings in my head. And then the next day, I go in and find myself second-guessing routine quality calls that I used to make without any trouble at all. It's just really starting to affect me, and I need to figure out how to handle it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding certain tasks or situations at work since the new operations manager started?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. I've actually started pulling back from meetings I'm expected to attend, just to avoid dealing with it. And because of that, I've missed some internal deadlines lately. My work record has really taken a hit, which is hard for me to admit. \n\nAnd it's spilling over, too. Like I mentioned, my husband and I are arguing a lot more, and I'm just... I'm not keeping up with our usual evening routines at home the way I used to. Everything just feels like too much right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any panic attacks since the new operations manager started?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't had anything like that. I mean, I get really stressed out and wound up, but... no, no actual panic attacks. Just a lot of worry.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or depressed most of the time since the new operations manager started?\n\nPatient: No, not really depressed. I mean, I'm definitely frustrated and overwhelmed by the whole situation, but I wouldn't say I feel sad most of the time. It's more just this constant tension and worrying about work, not a deep sadness.\n\nDoctor: Have you been repeating certain behaviors or mental acts in response to the stress at work?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed anything like that. I'm not doing anything over and over again, or... having to think certain things to cope. It's really just the stress and the avoiding things I mentioned before.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific about the new operations manager or the changes they've made that you think is causing you the most distress?\n\nPatient: I don't know if it's one specific thing. It's just... the whole way they run things now. Everything is different, the expectations feel impossible, and I just can't seem to get a handle on it. It's just the constant pressure of it all, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your symptoms would improve if the situation with the new operations manager were to resolve itself?\n\nPatient: Well, the situation hasn't actually ended yet. I still have to report to him every day and deal with all the new procedures he put in place. So, I don't really know if they would improve, because I haven't had a chance to see what it would be like if it was resolved. It's just ongoing, so I haven't had a period where the situation stopped and these problems just kept going on their own for months, you know?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these work-related stress symptoms?\n\nPatient: It's been going on for about two months now. It really started right around the time the new operations manager came in and began changing up all my inspection procedures.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. It's just... the whole way they run things now. Everything is different, the expectations feel impossible, and I just can't seem to get a handle on it. It's just the constant pressure of it all, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your symptoms would improve if the situation with the new operations manager were to resolve itself?\n\nPatient: Well, the situation hasn't actually ended yet. I still have to report to him every day and deal with all the new procedures he put in place. So, I don't really know if they would improve, because I haven't had a chance to see what it would be like if it was resolved. It's just ongoing, so I haven't had a period where the situation stopped and these problems just kept going on their own for months, you know?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these work-related stress symptoms?\n\nPatient: It's been going on for about two months now. It really started right around the time the new operations manager came in and began changing up all my inspection procedures.\n\nDoctor: Are you experiencing any repetitive thoughts or urges to perform specific rituals outside of your work environment?\n\nPatient: No, I don't have anything like that. I'd like to think I wouldn't be. I've been doing inspection work for a long time, and I never used to feel like this. It really just started when this new manager came in and changed all the procedures on us.

I'm not keeping up with our usual evening routines at home the way I used to. Everything just feels like too much right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any panic attacks since the new operations manager started?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't had anything like that. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I think it's really the combination of both, but it all started with him. Ever since this new operations manager came in and started picking apart the inspection procedures I've used for years, it's just... it's completely thrown me off. 

I can't seem to leave work at work anymore. I come home all wound up, I end up snapping at my husband over nothing, and I lie awake at night just replaying meetings in my head. And then the next day, because I'm so stressed and tired, I find myself second-guessing routine quality calls that I used to make without any trouble at all. So, it's the pressure, yes, but it's pressure that he brought with him.

I'm not keeping up with our usual evening routines at home the way I used to. It's really just the stress and the avoiding things I mentioned before.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific about the new operations manager or the changes they've made that you think is causing you the most distress?\n\nPatient: I don't know if it's one specific thing. It's just... the whole way they run things now. Everything is different, the expectations feel impossible, and I just can't seem to get a handle on it. It's just the constant pressure of it all, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your symptoms would improve if the situation with the new operations manager were to resolve itself?\n\nPatient: Well, the situation hasn't actually ended yet. I still have to report to him every day and deal with all the new procedures he put in place. So, I don't really know if they would improve, because I haven't had a chance to see what it would be like if it was resolved. It's just ongoing, so I haven't had a period where the situation stopped and these problems just kept going on their own for months, you know?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these work-related stress symptoms?\n\nPatient: It's been going on for about two months now. It really started right around the time the new operations manager came in and began changing up all my inspection procedures.\n\nDoctor: Are you experiencing any repetitive thoughts or urges to perform specific rituals outside of your work environment?\n\nPatient: No, I don't have anything like that. I don't have any urges to do rituals or... or have repetitive thoughts, no. When I'm at home, I'm really just trying to unwind from the day and get my mind off the plant. So, no, nothing like that going on.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would feel back to your normal self if you were to change jobs or the new operations manager were to leave?\n\nPatient: Well, like I mentioned a little bit ago, this situation hasn't really ended yet. I still have to report to him and deal with the procedures he changed. I'd like to think I'd feel back to my normal self if I got a different job or if he left, but I just don't know for sure. I haven't had any period after it was resolved where these problems kept going for months on their own to see what would happen.\n\nDoctor: Are you experiencing stress or anxiety in situations outside of work?\n\nPatient: No, not really. The stress is pretty much just tied to work. When I'm at home or out doing other things, I'm okay. It's really just the situation at the plant that's getting to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be stressed or anxious if you were in a similar work role, but with a different manager or work environment?\n\nPatient: I'd like to think I wouldn't be. I've been doing inspection work for a long time, and I never used to feel like this. It really just started when this new manager came in and changed all the procedures on us. \n\nSo... if I were doing the exact same job somewhere else, or working under someone different, I think I'd be okay. But I guess I don't know for absolute sure, since I haven't actually been in that situation yet. I just know that the way things are right now is what's causing the problem.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would experience similar stress and anxiety if you were to encounter a different stressful situation at work, unrelated to the new operations manager?\n\nPatient: I'm really not sure. I guess it would depend on what the situation was. I've been doing this a long time and I've always handled regular work stress just fine before all this started. So, um, I'd like to think I wouldn't get this worked up over something else, but... I just don't know for sure since I haven't really had that happen.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your stress and anxiety are specifically because of the new operations manager, or are they more about the changes and pressures that have come with the new job expectations?\n\nPatient: I think it's really the combination of both, but it all started with him. Ever since this new operations manager came in and started picking apart the inspection procedures I've used for years, it's just... it's completely thrown me off. \n\nI can't seem to leave work at work anymore. I come home all wound up, I end up snapping at my husband over nothing, and I lie awake at night just replaying meetings in my head. And then the next day, because I'm so stressed and tired, I find myself second-guessing routine quality calls that I used to make without any trouble at all. So, it's the pressure, yes, but it's pressure that he brought with him.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your symptoms would completely go away if the new operations manager and their changes were to suddenly disappear tomorrow?\n\nPatient: I really think they would. I mean, this whole situation hasn't ended yet—I still have to report to him every day and deal with all the procedures he changed. So I haven't really had a chance to see what would happen if it all just stopped. \n\nI haven't had any period where the issue was actually resolved and these problems just kept going on their own for months, you know? It's just a constant, ongoing thing right now. So, yes, if he and the changes disappeared tomorrow, I feel pretty confident I'd go back to normal.\n\nDoctor: Would your stress and anxiety completely go away if the changes to your job expectations remained, but you no longer had to work under the new operations manager?\n\nPatient: I'm honestly not sure. Um, it's hard to separate the two, because he's the one who brought all those changes in. \n\nIf he was gone but the new procedures stayed... I really don't know. Maybe it wouldn't be quite as bad without him constantly picking things apart, but the new expectations are still a big part of the pressure. So, I guess I just don't have a clear answer for that, since the two things kind of go hand in hand right now.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. It's really just the situation at the plant that's getting to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be stressed or anxious if you were in a similar work role, but with a different manager or work environment?\n\nPatient: I'd like to think I wouldn't be. So, um, I'd like to think I wouldn't get this worked up over something else, but... I just don't know for sure since I haven't really had that happen.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your stress and anxiety are specifically because of the new operations manager, or are they more about the changes and pressures that have come with the new job expectations?\n\nPatient: I think it's really the combination of both, but it all started with him. I come home all wound up, I end up snapping at my husband over nothing, and I lie awake at night just replaying meetings in my head. And then the next day, because I'm so stressed and tired, I find myself second-guessing routine quality calls that I used to make without any trouble at all. So, it's the pressure, yes, but it's pressure that he brought with him.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your symptoms would completely go away if the new operations manager and their changes were to suddenly disappear tomorrow?\n\nPatient: I really think they would. So I haven't really had a chance to see what would happen if it all just stopped. \n\nI haven't had any period where the issue was actually resolved and these problems just kept going on their own for months, you know? It's just a constant, ongoing thing right now. So, yes, if he and the changes disappeared tomorrow, I feel pretty confident I'd go back to normal.\n\nDoctor: Would your stress and anxiety completely go away if the changes to your job expectations remained, but you no longer had to work under the new operations manager?\n\nPatient: I'm honestly not sure. Um, it's hard to separate the two, because he's the one who brought all those changes in. \n\nIf he was gone but the new procedures stayed... I really don't know. Maybe it wouldn't be quite as bad without him constantly picking things apart, but the new expectations are still a big part of the pressure. So, I guess I just don't have a clear answer for that, since the two things kind of go hand in hand right now.\n\nDoctor: Would you experience any significant stress or anxiety if you were to work under a different manager with the same job expectations and procedures?\n\nPatient: I guess that's kind of the same thing I was just trying to figure out. I'm not sure if it would completely go away or not. I just haven't been in that situation to know for sure.\n\nDoctor: Would you say your stress and anxiety are only about the new operations manager, or are they also about the changes he has made to your job?\n\nPatient: It's definitely both.
